\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}(T^{\ell}w_{J}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon})\|_{C^{0}}+\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

(104)

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}T^{\ell}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}+\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}\|_{C^{0}}.

(105)

For the first term, we use the bounds of Lemma
3.5
for
u_{J}=T^{\ell}w_{J}
and the bounds in the Main Lemma for
\theta_{\epsilon}
:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}T^{\ell}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}S_{u}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))\cdot\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{u}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t)).

For the
\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}\|_{0}
term, a sufficient bound was already obtained in Proposition
3.3
.

For the high frequency bound, we recall the equation for
\bar{w}_{J}
(
51
):

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\bar{w}_{J}+T^{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}\nabla_{\ell}\bar{w}_{J}

\displaystyle=-T^{\ell}w_{J}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}-\tilde{O}_{J}

We apply
P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}
to both sides of the equation:

\displaystyle P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{w}_{J}

\displaystyle=-P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}(T^{\ell}w_{J}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon})-P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}

By the product rule,

\displaystyle P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{w}_{J}

\displaystyle=-\overset{\sim}{\sum}P_{q}(\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}T^{\ell}w_{J}\,\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon})-P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}

Then by the triangle inequality and the Holder inequality for the
C^{0}
norm,

\displaystyle\|P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{w}_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|P_{q}(\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}T^{\ell}w_{J}\,\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon})\|_{C^{0}}+\|P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

By the
C^{0}
bounds of Lemma
3.5
for
u_{J}=T^{\ell}w_{J}
, the bounds in (
63
) for
\theta_{\epsilon}
, and the
C^{0}
bound for
\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}\|_{0}
from Proposition
3.3
:

\displaystyle\|P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{w}_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\alpha q}\left(\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}u^{\ell}_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}+\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}u^{\ell}_{J}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\|P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\alpha q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}S_{u}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))\cdot\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-(L-1))_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+2^{-\alpha q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}S_{w}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))\cdot\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-(L-1))_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+2^{-\alpha q}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(2^{-\alpha q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{u}(1+\mathpzc{h})\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ +2^{-\alpha q}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\tau^{-1}S_{w}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\alpha q}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\tau^{-1}S_{w}(1+\mathpzc{h})\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}

where the second line follows from Lemma
3.5
and in the last line we recall
\tau^{-1}\sim_{L,\eta}(\log\widehat{\Xi})(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})
and
S_{u}=(\log\widehat{\Xi})S_{w}
. We have bounded the
\tilde{O}_{J}
term using Proposition
3.3
.

The bound we used on
\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}
above follows by the interpolation
\|F\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|F\|_{C^{0}}^{1-\alpha}\|\nabla F\|_{C^{0}}^{\alpha}
with
F=\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}
, which yields

\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|+1-L)_{+}}\widehat{N}^{\alpha}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|+1+\alpha}e_{u}^{1/2}

Thus

\displaystyle\|P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{w}_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\alpha q}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{u}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-\alpha q}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\tau^{-1}S_{w}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t)).

∎

For the purpose of estimating the velocity increment
u_{J}^{\ell}=T^{\ell}w_{J}
and
\rho_{J}={\cal R}^{j\ell}w_{J}
, we will use the following estimates, with
{\cal Q}
being either
T
or
\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}
:

Lemma 3.6
.

Suppose
{\cal Q}
is a Fourier-multiplier with a degree zero homogeneous symbol that is smooth away from the origin. Then for any
F\in\{w_{J}\}\cup\{r_{J}\}
and
|\vec{a}|\leq L^{\prime}

\displaystyle\overline{D}_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal Q}F(t)

\displaystyle={\cal Q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}F(t)+(\log\widehat{\Xi})\,O\left(\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{F}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))\right)

(106)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\overline{D}_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal Q}P_{q}F(t)&=P_{q}{\cal Q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}F(t)\\
&+\min\{1,2^{-\alpha q}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\}\,O\left(\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{F}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))\right)\end{split}

(107)

(Note that
P_{q}
commutes with
\nabla_{\vec{a}}
and with
{\cal Q}
but not with
\overline{D}_{t}
.)

Proof of (
107
).

Start with

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}F

\displaystyle=\overline{D}_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}}F+[\nabla_{\vec{a}},\overline{D}_{t}]F

\displaystyle[\nabla_{\vec{a}},\overline{D}_{t}]F

\displaystyle=\overset{\sim}{\sum}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}u_{\epsilon}^{i}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{i}F\,1_{|\vec{a}_{1}|\geq 1}

where the sum is over certain
{\vec{a}_{1},\vec{a}_{2}}
with
|\vec{a}_{1}|+|\vec{a}_{2}|=|\vec{a}|
.
Now apply the operator
{\cal Q}_{q}={\cal Q}P_{q}
to obtain

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}F

\displaystyle=\overline{D}_{t}{\cal Q}_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}F+[{\cal Q}_{q},\overline{D}_{t}]\nabla_{\vec{a}}F+{\cal Q}_{q}[\nabla_{\vec{a}},\overline{D}_{t}]F

We start with the third term.

Since
{\cal Q}_{q}
localizes an order
0
operator to frequency
2^{q}
, we have
\|Q_{q}[f]\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\min\{\|f\|_{C^{0}},2^{-\alpha q}\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\}
, hence

\displaystyle\|{\cal Q}_{q}[\nabla_{\vec{a}},\overline{D}_{t}]F\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}u_{\epsilon}^{i}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{i}F\|_{C^{0}}1_{|\vec{a}_{2}|\leq L^{\prime}-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}[\widehat{N}^{((|\vec{a}_{1}|-1)+1-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}e_{u}^{1/2}]1_{|\vec{a}_{1}|-1\geq 0}[\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}S_{F}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))]

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{F}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))

where in the last line we applied the counting inequality with
(|\vec{a}_{1}|-1,|\vec{a}_{2}|+3,{\underline{L}})
all
\geq 0
.
We also have

\displaystyle\|{\cal Q}_{q}[\nabla_{\vec{a}},\overline{D}_{t}]F\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\alpha q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}(\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}u_{\epsilon}^{i}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{i}F\|_{C^{0}}+\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}u_{\epsilon}^{i}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{i}F\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}})1_{|\vec{a}_{2}|\leq L^{\prime}-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}2^{-\alpha q}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}[\widehat{N}^{((|\vec{a}_{1}|-1)+1-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}e_{u}^{1/2}]1_{|\vec{a}_{1}|-1\geq 0}[\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}S_{F}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))]

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\alpha q}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{\vec{a}}S_{F}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))

We conclude by estimating

\displaystyle[\overline{D}_{t},{\cal Q}_{q}]\nabla_{\vec{a}}F

\displaystyle=\int(u_{\epsilon}^{j}(x-h)-u^{j}_{\epsilon}(x))\nabla_{j}\nabla_{\vec{a}}F(x-h)Q_{q}(h)dh

\displaystyle=-\int(u_{\epsilon}^{j}(x-h)-u^{j}_{\epsilon}(x))\nabla_{j}^{(h)}\nabla_{\vec{a}}F(x-h)Q_{q}(h)dh

\displaystyle=-\int(u_{\epsilon}^{j}(x-h)-u^{j}_{\epsilon}(x))\nabla_{j}^{(h)}(\nabla_{\vec{a}}F(x-h)-\nabla_{\vec{a}}F(x))Q_{q}(h)dh

\displaystyle=\int(u_{\epsilon}^{j}(x-h)-u^{j}_{\epsilon}(x))(\nabla_{\vec{a}}F(x-h)-\nabla_{\vec{a}}F(x))\nabla_{j}Q_{q}(h)dh

\displaystyle\|[\overline{D}_{t},{\cal Q}_{q}]\nabla_{\vec{a}}F\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}F\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\int|h|^{1+\alpha}|\nabla Q_{q}(h)|dh

\displaystyle\lesssim[\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}][\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{F}(\mathpzc{h}(t)+1)\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}][2^{-\alpha q}]

and a similar integration by parts yields

\displaystyle[\overline{D}_{t},{\cal Q}_{q}]\nabla_{\vec{a}}F

\displaystyle=\int(u_{\epsilon}^{j}(x-h)-u^{j}_{\epsilon}(x))\nabla_{j}\nabla_{\vec{a}}F(x-h)Q_{q}(h)dh

\displaystyle=-\int(u_{\epsilon}^{j}(x-h)-u^{j}_{\epsilon}(x))\nabla_{j}^{(h)}\nabla_{\vec{a}}F(x-h)Q_{q}(h)dh

\displaystyle\|[\overline{D}_{t},{\cal Q}_{q}]\nabla_{\vec{a}}F\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle=\|\nabla u_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}F\|_{C^{0}}\int|h|^{1}|\nabla Q_{q}(h)|dh

\displaystyle\lesssim[\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}][\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{F}(\mathpzc{h}(t)+1)][1].

Combining these bounds concludes the proof.

∎

Proof of (
106
).

For
|\vec{a}|\leq L^{\prime}
write

\displaystyle[\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal Q},\overline{D}_{t}]F

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}[\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal Q}_{q},\overline{D}_{t}]F

\displaystyle\|[\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal Q},\overline{D}_{t}]F\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q=0}^{\hat{q}-1}\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}[\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{F}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))]

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=\hat{q}}^{\infty}2^{-\alpha q}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}[\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{F}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))]

Since
\hat{q}\lesssim\log\widehat{\Xi}
and
2^{-\alpha\hat{q}}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\lesssim 1
, we obtain the desired bound.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.5
for
\bar{z}_{J}
.

We need to show that for all
|\vec{a}|\leq L^{\prime}
and all
q>\hat{q}
, we have the bounds

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{z}_{J}(s)\|_{C^{0}}+2^{\alpha q}\widehat{\Xi}^{-\alpha}\|P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{z}_{J}(s)\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\tau^{-1}S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))

(108)

For the
C^{0}
bound on
\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{z}_{J}
, we use the equation (
55
):

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{z}_{J}^{i}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}(\nabla_{a}T^{i}\theta_{\epsilon}z_{J}^{a}-T^{i}w_{J}\theta_{\epsilon}-\tilde{O}_{J}^{i})\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla_{a}T^{i}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}z_{J}^{a}\|_{C^{0}}+\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}T^{i}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}+\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}^{i}\|_{C^{0}}.

For the first term, we use the bounds for
\theta_{\epsilon}
from Lemma
3.7
and the inductive hypothesis for
z_{J}
:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla_{a}T^{i}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}z_{J}^{a}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|+1-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}\cdot\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t)).

For the second term, we use the bounds (
72
) for
u_{J}^{i}=T^{i}w_{J}
:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}T^{i}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}S_{u}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))\cdot\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}e_{u}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))\log\widehat{\Xi},

The
\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}^{i}\|_{0}
term was already bounded in Proposition
3.3
. We have

\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}^{i}_{J}\|_{0}\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\tau^{-1}S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))

The bound on the high frequency projection
P_{q}
is proved very similarly to the high frequency bound for
\bar{w}_{J}
. The key point is that taking a
\dot{C}^{\alpha}
norm costs at most
\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}
.

∎

Proof of Proposition
3.5
for
\bar{r}_{J}
.

Recall that
\bar{r}_{J}
satisfies the equation (
59
):

\displaystyle\overline{D}_{t}\bar{r}_{J}^{ij}=\partial_{t}\bar{r}_{J}^{ij}+T^{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}\nabla_{\ell}\bar{r}_{J}^{ij}

\displaystyle={\cal R}^{ij}_{a}\nabla_{\ell}[\nabla_{b}T^{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}r_{J}^{ab}]+{\cal B}^{ij}_{b}[z_{J}^{b},\theta_{\epsilon}]-\tilde{O}_{J}^{ij}

(109)

We need to show that for all
|\vec{a}|\leq L^{\prime}
, we have the bounds

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{r}_{J}(s)\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-\underline{L})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\tau^{-1}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h}(t))

(110)

For the
C^{0}
bound (
110
), we need to bound

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}({\cal R}^{ij}_{a}\nabla_{\ell}[\nabla_{b}T^{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}r_{J}^{ab}])+\nabla_{\vec{a}}({\cal B}^{ij}_{b}[z_{J}^{b},\theta_{\epsilon}])-\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{O}_{J}^{ij}.

For the first term, we use Proposition
3.6
to obtain the bound

\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}({\cal R}^{ij}_{a}\nabla_{\ell}[\nabla_{b}T^{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}r_{J}^{ab}])\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim(\log\widehat{\Xi})\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}(\nabla u_{\epsilon}r_{J})\|_{C^{0}}+\widehat{\Xi}^{-\alpha}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}(\nabla u_{\epsilon}r_{J})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}.

Then

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}(\nabla u_{\epsilon}r_{J})\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|+1-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}\cdot S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})

and thus
(\log\widehat{\Xi})\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}(\nabla u_{\epsilon}r_{J})\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\tau^{-1}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})
.

Using the product rule for
\dot{C}^{\alpha}
norms, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}(\nabla u_{\epsilon}r_{J})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla u_{\epsilon}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}r_{J}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}(\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla u_{\epsilon}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}r_{J}\|_{C^{0}}+\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla u_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}r_{J}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}).

For the first term in the sum, we use the interpolation inequality for Hölder norms to get

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla u_{\epsilon}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla u_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}^{1-\alpha}\|\nabla\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla u_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}^{\alpha}\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|+2-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}r_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h}).

Thus, the first term is bounded by

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla u_{\epsilon}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}r_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})

For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla u_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|+1-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2},

and by the definition of
\mathpzc{h}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}r_{J}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}.

Thus, the second term is bounded by

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla u_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}r_{J}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|+1-L)_{+}+(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}.

Combining these estimates, we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}(\nabla u_{\epsilon}r_{J})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}.

Therefore, using Proposition
3.6
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\Xi}^{-\alpha}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}(\nabla u_{\epsilon}r_{J})\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\tau^{-1}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h}),

where the last inequality uses
\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}\lesssim\tau^{-1}
. We conclude

\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}({\cal R}^{ij}_{a}\nabla_{\ell}[\nabla_{b}T^{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}r_{J}^{ab}])\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\tau^{-1}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h}).

For
q\geq\hat{q}
we must also bound
P_{q}
of this term. To do so, we recall the estimate on the
\dot{C}^{\alpha}
norm that we just proved to obtain

\displaystyle\|P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}({\cal R}^{ij}_{a}\nabla_{\ell}[\nabla_{b}T^{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}r_{J}^{ab}])\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\alpha q}\|[\nabla_{b}T^{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}r_{J}^{ab}]\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\alpha q}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\tau^{-1}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h}),

which is our desired bound.

It now remains to estimate the other two terms. For the forcing term
\tilde{O}^{ij}
the desired estimates follow directly from Proposition (
3.3
) and the Littlewood Paley characterization of the
\dot{C}^{\alpha}
norm. A more involved analysis is necessary for the
{\cal B}
term.

The
{\cal B}
term

This section is one of the main novelties of our analysis. We now define and estimate the
{\cal B}
term, which is required to satistfy

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{a}[z_{J}^{a},\theta]

\displaystyle=\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[z_{J}^{a}]\theta-z_{J}^{a}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[\theta]

Observe that the vector field on the right hand side can be written as

\displaystyle=\sum_{q}

\displaystyle\left[\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[P_{\leq q+1}z_{J}^{a}]P_{\leq q+1}\theta-P_{\leq q+1}z_{J}^{a}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[P_{\leq q+1}\theta]\right]

(111)

\displaystyle-\left[\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[P_{\leq q}z_{J}^{a}]P_{\leq q}\theta-P_{\leq q}z_{J}^{a}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[P_{\leq q}\theta]\right]

(112)

We now decompose the right hand side as a paraproduct

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[z_{J}^{a}]\theta-z_{J}^{a}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[\theta]=LH+HL+HH

\displaystyle LH

\displaystyle=\sum_{q}P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[z_{J}^{a}]P_{q+1}\theta-P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[\theta]P_{q+1}z_{J}^{a}

\displaystyle HL

\displaystyle=\sum_{q}P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[z_{J}^{a}]P_{\leq q-1}\theta-P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[\theta]P_{\leq q-1}z_{J}^{a}

\displaystyle HH

\displaystyle=\sum_{q}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[P_{q+1}z_{J}^{a}]P_{q+1}\theta-\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[P_{q+1}\theta]P_{q+1}z_{J}^{a}

\displaystyle+\sum_{q}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[P_{q+1}z_{J}^{a}]P_{q}\theta-\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[P_{q}\theta]P_{q+1}z_{J}^{a}

\displaystyle+\sum_{q}\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[P_{q}z_{J}^{a}]P_{q+1}\theta-\nabla_{a}T^{\ell}[P_{q+1}\theta]P_{q}z_{J}^{a}

Note that the
HL
and
LH
terms both live at frequency
2^{q}
. For these we apply an order
-1
operator
\mathcal{R}_{a}^{j\ell}
that solves the divergence equation. For the high-high terms, we invoke the divergence form principle of Section
A.2
(in particular the fact that the multiplier for
\nabla T
is
even
and the fact that a minus sign appears) to write them as the divergence of a bilinear convolution. Hence, for an appropriate Fourier multiplier
P_{\approx q}
with symbol equal to
1
on a neighborhood of
|\xi|=2^{q}
,

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{a}[z_{J}^{a},\theta]

\displaystyle={\cal B}^{j\ell}_{H}+{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{LH}+{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{HL}

(113)

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{LH}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q}P_{\approx q}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{b}[P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[z_{J}^{a}]P_{q+1}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[\theta_{\epsilon}]P_{q+1}z_{J}^{a}]

(114)

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{HL}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q}P_{\approx q}\mathcal{R}_{b}^{j\ell}[P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[z_{J}^{a}]P_{\leq q-1}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[\theta_{\epsilon}]P_{\leq q-1}z_{J}^{a}]

(115)

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{H}

\displaystyle=HH

(116)

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{H}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q}K_{qa}^{j\ell}\ast[z_{J}^{a},\theta_{\epsilon}]=\sum_{q}K_{qa}^{j\ell}\ast[P_{\approx q}z_{J}^{a},P_{\approx q}\theta_{\epsilon}]

(117)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}z_{J}^{a}(x-h_{1})\theta_{\epsilon}(x-h_{2})K_{qa}^{j\ell}(h_{1},h_{2})\,dh_{1}dh_{2}

(118)

where
K_{qa}^{j\ell}(h_{1},h_{2})
is a Schwartz function on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
and

\displaystyle K_{qa}^{j\ell}(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle=2^{4q}K_{0a}^{j\ell}(2^{q}h_{1},2^{q}h_{2}).

(119)

We begin by estimating the high-high term. We decompose into high and low frequencies, observing that the spatial derivatives commute with the bilinear convolution kernel

\displaystyle\underline{{\cal B}}_{H}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{q=0}^{\hat{q}-1}K_{qa}^{j\ell}\ast[z_{J}^{a},\theta_{\epsilon}]

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}\underline{{\cal B}}_{H}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle=\overset{\sim}{\sum}\sum_{q=0}^{\hat{q}-1}K_{qa}^{j\ell}\ast[\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}z_{J}^{a},\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}]

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\underline{{\cal B}}_{H}^{j\ell}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q=0}^{\hat{q}-1}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|K_{qa}^{j\ell}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}z_{J}^{a}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q=0}^{\hat{q}}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h})[\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}e_{u}^{1/2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log\widehat{\Xi})\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\qquad\mbox{since }S_{z}=\Xi S_{r}

For the high frequencies we bound

\displaystyle\overline{{\cal B}}_{H}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{q=\hat{q}}^{\infty}K_{qa}^{j\ell}\ast[P_{\approx q}z_{J}^{a},P_{\approx q}\theta]

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{{\cal B}}_{H}^{j\ell}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q=\hat{q}}^{\infty}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|K_{qa}^{j\ell}\|_{L^{1}}\|P_{\approx q}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}z_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q=\hat{q}}^{\infty}\overset{\sim}{\sum}2^{-\alpha q}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}z_{J}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{\Xi}^{-\alpha}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}(\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h}))(\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}e_{u}^{1/2})

\displaystyle\lesssim(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\qquad\mbox{since }S_{z}=\Xi S_{r}

Finally, for
q^{\prime}>\hat{q}
, we bound
P_{q^{\prime}}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}_{H}^{j\ell}
by observing that, due to frequency truncation, only terms with
q>q^{\prime}-2
can contribute. That is, from the formula

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}P_{\approx q}z_{J}^{a}(x-h_{1})P_{\approx q}\theta_{\epsilon}(x-h_{2})K_{qa}^{j\ell}(h_{1},h_{2})\,dh_{1}dh_{2}

we see that the biconvolution only translates each factor in physical space and therefore modulates in frequency space. The integral above will still be localized to frequencies below
2^{q+2}
since the Fourier transform maps products to convolutions. Therefore, we are able to bound

\displaystyle P_{q^{\prime}}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}_{H}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=q^{\prime}-3}^{\infty}P_{q^{\prime}}\overset{\sim}{\sum}K_{qa}^{j\ell}\ast[P_{\approx q}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}z_{J}^{a},P_{\approx q}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}]

\displaystyle\|P_{q^{\prime}}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}_{H}^{j\ell}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q^{\prime}-3}^{\infty}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|P_{\approx q}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}z_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q^{\prime}-3}^{\infty}2^{-\alpha q}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}z_{J}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\alpha q^{\prime}}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h})e_{u}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\alpha q^{\prime}}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\qquad\mbox{since }S_{z}=\Xi S_{r}.

Proof for the
\mathcal{B}
Term, Part 2 (High-Low terms): The philosophy of the High-Low term estimates is similar to the gluing in
[
37
]
. Namely, for low frequencies we substitute
z_{J}^{j}=\nabla_{i}r_{J}^{ij}
while for high frequencies we use our bounds on
w_{J}
and
z_{J}
.

Specifically, we split the High-Low term into high frequency and low frequency components

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{HL}

\displaystyle={\cal B}^{j\ell}_{HLH}+{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{HLL}

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{HLH}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q>\bar{q}}P_{\approx q}\mathcal{R}_{b}^{j\ell}[P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[z_{J}^{a}]P_{\leq q-1}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[\theta_{\epsilon}]P_{\leq q-1}z_{J}^{a}]

(120)

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{HLL}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=0}^{\bar{q}}P_{\approx q}\mathcal{R}_{b}^{j\ell}[P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[z_{J}^{a}]P_{\leq q-1}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[\theta_{\epsilon}]P_{\leq q-1}z_{J}^{a}]

(121)

Here
\bar{q}
is chosen so that
2^{\bar{q}}\sim\Xi
.

We start with the
HLH
term.

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\eqref{eq:HLHterm}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q>\bar{q}}2^{-q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|P_{q+1}T^{b}[\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla_{a}z_{J}^{a}]\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}P_{\leq q-1}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

(122)

\displaystyle+\sum_{q>\bar{q}}2^{-q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|P_{q+1}T^{b}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\nabla_{a}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\|P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}z_{J}^{a}\|_{C^{0}}

(123)

We substitute
\nabla_{a}z_{J}^{a}=w_{J}
and use the bounds
\|P_{\approx q}{\cal R}_{b}^{j\ell}\|\lesssim 2^{-q}
,
\|P_{\leq q-1}\|\lesssim 1,\|P_{q+1}T\|\lesssim 1
as operators on
C^{0}
to obtain

(
122
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q>\bar{q}}2^{-q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|P_{q+1}T^{b}[\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}w_{J}]\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}P_{\leq q-1}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

(124)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-\bar{q}}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}S_{w}(1+\mathpzc{h})\cdot\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}e_{u}^{1/2}

(125)

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\qquad\mbox{since }S_{w}=\Xi^{2}S_{r}

(126)

The other term is bounded by the same quantity

(
123
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q>\bar{q}}2^{-q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-(L-1))_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}e_{u}^{1/2}\cdot\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h})

(127)

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\qquad\mbox{since }S_{z}=\Xi S_{r}

(128)

We now treat the low-frequency term by recalling
z_{J}^{a}=\nabla_{c}r_{J}^{ca}
and using the bounds
\|P_{q}\nabla^{L}T\|\lesssim 2^{Lq}
,
\|P_{\leq q-1}\nabla\|\lesssim 2^{q}
as operators on
C^{0}
:

(
121
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=0}^{\bar{q}}P_{\approx q}\mathcal{R}_{b}^{j\ell}[P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[\nabla_{c}r_{J}^{ca}]P_{\leq q-1}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[\theta_{\epsilon}]P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{c}r_{J}^{ca}]

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\eqref{eq:HLLterm}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q}2^{-q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}\nabla_{c}\|\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}r_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{q}2^{-q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|P_{q+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}\|\|P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{c}\|\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}r_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q}2^{q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}r_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi\cdot\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})e_{u}^{1/2}=\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})

This bound is what is desired for the low frequency contributions.

For all
q\geq\hat{q}
, we need to also bound the Littlewood Paley projections of
{\cal B}_{HL}^{j\ell}
. By frequency support considerations, note that

\displaystyle P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{HL}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q^{\prime}=q-2}^{q+2}P_{\approx q^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}_{b}^{j\ell}P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}[P_{q^{\prime}+1}T^{b}[w_{J}]P_{\leq q^{\prime}-1}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{q^{\prime}+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[\theta_{\epsilon}]P_{\leq q^{\prime}-1}z_{J}^{a}]

(129)

Then since
\|P_{q^{\prime}}{\cal R}\|\lesssim 2^{-q}\lesssim\Xi^{-1}
we have

(
129
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi^{-1}2^{-\alpha q}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}[P_{q^{\prime}+1}T^{b}[w_{J}]P_{\leq q^{\prime}-1}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{q^{\prime}+1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[\theta_{\epsilon}]P_{\leq q^{\prime}-1}z_{J}^{a}]\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

(130)

Note that the estimate for the
HLH
term bounded the
C^{0}
norm of the right hand side rather than the
\dot{C}^{\alpha}
seminorm. It is clear from the Littlewood-Paley characterization of the seminorm that
\|Tf\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}
. Using this information, we see that taking the
\dot{C}^{\alpha}
seminorm costs at most
\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}
compared to the estimate for the
C^{0}
norm, which is the bound we require.

We are left with the
LH
term, which we decompose as

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{j\ell}_{LH}

\displaystyle={\cal B}_{LHL}^{j\ell}+{\cal B}_{LHH}^{j\ell}

(131)

\displaystyle{\cal B}_{LHL}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=0}^{\bar{q}}P_{\approx q}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{b}[P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[z_{J}^{a}]P_{q+1}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[\theta_{\epsilon}]P_{q+1}z_{J}^{a}]

(132)

\displaystyle{\cal B}_{LHH}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q>\bar{q}}P_{\approx q}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{b}[P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[z_{J}^{a}]P_{q+1}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}[\theta_{\epsilon}]P_{q+1}z_{J}^{a}]

(133)

For the
LHL
term, we substitute
z_{J}^{a}=\nabla_{c}r_{J}^{ca}
and use
\|P_{\leq q}\nabla^{L}T\|\lesssim 2^{Lq}
for
L=1,2
(which follows by dyadic decomposition) as well as
\|P_{\approx q}\mathcal{R}\|\lesssim 2^{-q}
, to obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}_{LHL}^{j\ell}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q=0}^{\bar{q}}2^{-q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\left(\|P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{c}T^{b}\|+\|P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{a}T^{b}\|\|P_{q+1}\nabla_{c}\|\right)\|\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}r_{J}^{ca}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q=0}^{\bar{q}}2^{q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}r_{J}^{ca}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})e_{u}^{1/2}=\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})

For the
LHH
term, we substitute
T^{b}\nabla_{a}z_{J}^{a}=T^{b}w_{J}=u_{J}^{b}
and
T^{b}\theta_{\epsilon}=u_{\epsilon}^{b}
, and use (
102
) to obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}_{LHH}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q>\bar{q}}\overset{\sim}{\sum}P_{\approx q}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{b}[P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}u_{J}^{b}P_{q+1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{a}u_{\epsilon}^{b}]P_{q+1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}z_{J}^{a}]

(134)

\displaystyle\|\eqref{eq:readyForLHH}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q>\bar{q}}2^{-q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\left([\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{u}(1+\mathpzc{h})][\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}e_{u}^{1/2}]\right.

\displaystyle\left.+[\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|+1-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}][\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h})]\right)

\displaystyle\|\eqref{eq:readyForLHH}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q>\bar{q}}2^{-q}(\log\widehat{\Xi})\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})S_{z}(1+\mathpzc{h})\qquad\mbox{since }S_{u}=(\log\widehat{\Xi})\Xi S_{z}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\log\widehat{\Xi})(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})S_{r}(1+\mathpzc{h})\qquad\mbox{since }S_{z}=\Xi S_{r}\mbox{ and }2^{-\bar{q}}\sim\Xi^{-1}

Finally we need to bound
P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}_{LHH}^{j\ell}
. Applying
P_{q}
to (
134
), we have

\displaystyle\|P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}_{LHH}^{j\ell}\|_{C^{0}}=

\displaystyle=\sum_{q^{\prime}=q-2}^{q+2}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|P_{\approx q^{\prime}}{\cal R}_{b}^{j\ell}\|\cdot 2^{-\alpha q}\|P_{\leq q^{\prime}-1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}u_{J}^{b}\,P_{q^{\prime}+1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{\leq q^{\prime}-1}[\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{a}u_{\epsilon}^{b}]\,P_{q^{\prime}+1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}z_{J}^{a}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi^{-1}\cdot 2^{-\alpha q}\sum_{q^{\prime}=q-2}^{q+2}\|P_{\leq q^{\prime}-1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}u_{J}^{b}\,P_{q^{\prime}+1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\theta_{\epsilon}-P_{\leq q^{\prime}-1}[\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{a}u_{\epsilon}^{b}]\,P_{q^{\prime}+1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}z_{J}^{a}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

This term can now be bounded using the method to estimate
\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}_{LHH}^{j\ell}\|_{C^{0}}
, with the extra input that a
\dot{C}^{\alpha}
seminorm costs an extra factor of
\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}
compared to the
C^{0}
norm. With this estimate we conclude the bound for
\bar{r}_{J}
. ∎

Now that we have estimated all of
{\bf F}_{J}=\{\bar{w}_{J},\bar{z}_{J},\bar{r}_{J}\}
, Proposition
3.4
guarantees that

\mathpzc{h}(t)\lesssim 1

is bounded on
|t-\tau k|\leq\tau
. We may now use this bound in the estimates that follow.

Proof of (
72
) and (
73
) for
u_{J}
.

Lemma
3.6
applied to
\nabla_{\vec{a}}w_{J}
and (
7
) give

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}T^{\ell}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log\widehat{\Xi})\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}+\widehat{\Xi}^{-\alpha}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}w_{J}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

(135)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log\widehat{\Xi})\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{w}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log\widehat{\Xi})\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\Xi^{1/2}D_{R}^{1/2}N^{-1/2}\ll(\log\widehat{\Xi})^{-1}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}e_{u}^{1/2}

The bound (
72
) follows from the estimates for
w_{J}
by Lemma
3.6

We now consider
\overline{D}_{t}u_{J}
and its bound (
73
). By the commutator estimate of Lemma
3.6
, it suffices to estimate
\|T^{\ell}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}
once we check that

\displaystyle(\log\widehat{\Xi})N^{(|\vec{a}|-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})S_{w}

\displaystyle\leq N^{(|\vec{a}|+1-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})e_{u}^{1/2}.

This inequality follows from the lower bound (
7
) and
S_{w}=\Xi^{2}\mu^{-1}D_{R,n}\leq\Xi^{1/2}D_{R}^{1/2}N^{-1/2}
.

It now suffices to obtain the same upper bound for

\displaystyle\|T^{i}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\|T^{i}[\nabla_{\vec{a}}[T^{\ell}w_{J}\nabla_{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}]\|_{C^{0}}

(136)

\displaystyle+\|T^{i}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{\ell}\nabla_{\vec{a}}A_{(f,J)}^{j\ell}\|_{C^{0}}

(137)

Term (
136
) is bounded using the product rule, the lower bound (
7
) and (
101
) (the fact that applying
T
costs a log) by

(
136
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}_{\vec{a}_{1},\vec{a}_{2}}(\log\widehat{\Xi})[(\log\widehat{\Xi})\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}S_{w}]\cdot[\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})(\log\widehat{\Xi})^{2}(\Xi^{1/2}D_{R}^{1/2}N^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})e_{u}^{1/2}

For the term (
137
) we decompose into high and low frequencies. Choosing an integer
\bar{q}
such that
2^{\bar{q}}\sim\Xi
, we separate

(
137
)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q=0}^{\bar{q}}\|T^{i}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{\ell}P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}A_{(f,J)}\|_{C^{0}}

(138)

\displaystyle+\sum_{q>\bar{q}}\|T^{i}P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{\ell}A_{(f,J)}^{j\ell}\|_{C^{0}}

(139)

The lower frequency term we estimate using the bound
\|T^{i}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{\ell}P_{q}\|\lesssim 2^{2q}
on the operator norm to obtain

(
138
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi^{2}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}D_{R,n}\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})e_{u}^{1/2}

The high frequency term is a bit more subtle. Recalling
e_{u}^{1/2}=\Xi^{1/2}D_{u}^{1/2}\geq\Xi^{1/2}D_{R}^{1/2}
, we have by (
84
) that

(
139
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q>\bar{q}}2^{-\alpha q}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{\ell}A_{(f,J)}^{j\ell}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q>\bar{q}}2^{-\alpha q}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+2+\alpha-(L-1))_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+2+\alpha}D_{R,n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})e_{u}^{1/2}

Note that factors of
\widehat{N}
do not appear until one takes more than
L-1
derivatives of
A_{(f,J)}
, thus explaining the power of
\widehat{N}
above.

∎

Proof of (
72
) and (
73
) for
u_{n}
.

Recall that the change in velocity due to one step of the Newton iteration is
u_{n}^{\ell}=T^{\ell}w_{n}=\sum_{k}\tilde{\chi}_{k}(t)u_{(k,n)}^{\ell}
. Inequalities (
72
) and (
73
) for
u_{n}^{\ell}
therefore follow directly follow from the same inequalities for the
u_{J}^{\ell}=u_{(k,n)}^{\ell}
once we bound the term where the advective derivative hits
\tilde{\chi}_{k}(t)
. This term has size

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\chi}_{k}^{\prime}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}u_{(k,n)}^{\ell}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau^{-1}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{u}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}(\log\widehat{\Xi})(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\log\widehat{\Xi})\Xi^{1/2}D_{R}^{1/2}N^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})e_{u}^{1/2}

as desired, using the lower bound (
7
) on
N
.
∎

Proof of (
74
) for
\rho_{J}
.

Recall the following bounds on
r_{J}
from Proposition
3.1
:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}r_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{a}}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{r},

(140)

For
\rho_{J}^{j\ell}={\cal R}^{j\ell}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}r_{J}^{ab}
, we thus obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\rho_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log\widehat{\Xi})\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}r_{J}\|_{0}+\widehat{\Xi}^{-\alpha}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}r_{J}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log\widehat{\Xi})\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{r}+\widehat{\Xi}^{-\alpha}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha}\mathpzc{h}S_{r}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{\rho}(1+\mathpzc{h})\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{\rho}.

To bound the advective derivative of
\rho_{J}
with a cost of
\tau^{-1}
rather than
\mu
we must examine the evolution equation for
r_{J}
. The crucial point is that the forcing term
A_{J}^{j\ell}
vanishes on the support of
\tilde{\chi}^{\prime}
.

Lemma
3.6
gives

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\rho_{J}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle={\cal R}^{j\ell}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}\overline{D}_{t}r_{J}^{ab}+(\log\widehat{\Xi})O(\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{r})

The term in the
O(\cdot)
is acceptable since
\tau^{-1}\sim\log\widehat{\Xi}\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}
and
S_{\rho}=(\log\widehat{\Xi})S_{r}
. For the first term, let us define the order zero operators
{\cal Q}^{j\ell}_{ab}={\cal R}^{j\ell}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}
and also
\tilde{\cal Q}^{j\ell}_{cd}={\cal Q}^{j\ell}_{ab}{\cal R}^{ab}_{c}\nabla_{d}
. We return to the equation for
r_{J}
to obtain, for
t\in\mathrm{supp}\,\partial_{t}\chi_{k}
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal Q}^{j\ell}_{ab}\overline{D}_{t}r_{J}^{ab}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal Q}^{j\ell}_{ab}{\cal R}^{ab}_{c}\nabla_{d}[\nabla_{e}T^{d}\theta_{\epsilon}r_{J}^{ce}]+\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal Q}^{j\ell}_{ab}{\cal B}^{ab}_{c}[z_{J}^{c},\theta_{\epsilon}]

(141)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\cal Q}^{j\ell}_{cd}\nabla_{\vec{a}}[\nabla_{e}T^{d}\theta_{\epsilon}r_{J}^{ce}]+{\cal Q}^{j\ell}_{ab}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}^{ab}_{c}[z_{J}^{c},\theta_{\epsilon}]

(142)

Note that the latter equation has exactly the same form as the equation (
109
) for
\bar{r}_{J}
except for the additional zeroth order operator
{\cal Q}_{ab}^{j\ell}
appearing in front of
{\cal B}^{ab}_{c}
. Thus we can repeat the analysis that was done for
\bar{r}_{J}
and use the inequality

\displaystyle\|{\cal Q}^{j\ell}_{ab}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}^{ab}_{c}[z_{J}^{c},\theta_{\epsilon}]\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log\widehat{\Xi}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}^{ab}_{c}[z_{J}^{c},\theta_{\epsilon}]\|_{C^{0}}+\widehat{\Xi}^{-\alpha}\sup_{q\geq\hat{q}}2^{\alpha q}\|P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}{\cal B}^{ab}_{c}[z_{J}^{c},\theta_{\epsilon}]\|_{C^{0}}.

Doing so we conclude that the estimate for
\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\rho_{J}
on
\mathrm{supp}\,\tilde{\chi}^{\prime}_{k}
is the same as the estimate for
\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\bar{r}_{J}
, but with a loss of one power of
\log\widehat{\Xi}
that comes from the presence of the additional zeroth order operator in front of
\mathcal{B}_{c}^{ab}
in (
142
). We omit the remaining details.
∎

Let us now conclude the proof of Proposition
3.1
.

Proof of (
71
).

Let
0\leq|\vec{a}|\leq 1
. Then

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}|\nabla|^{-1/2}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q\leq\bar{q}}\||\nabla|^{-1/2}P_{q}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{\vec{a}}z_{J}^{i}\|_{C^{0}}+\sum_{q\geq\bar{q}}\||\nabla|^{-1/2}P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q\leq\bar{q}}2^{q/2}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}z_{J}\|_{C^{0}}+\sum_{q\geq\bar{q}}2^{-q/2}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi^{1/2}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}[\Xi^{-1}S_{w}]+\Xi^{-1/2}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{w}

\displaystyle\sim\Xi^{-1/2}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}S_{w}\lesssim\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}D_{R,n}^{1/2}.

The desired bound for
w_{n}=\sum_{k,n}\tilde{\chi}w_{k,n}
now follows.
∎

Proof of (
75
), (
76
), and (
77
).

We first prove (
75
). We have

\displaystyle R_{(n+1)}=\sum_{k}\tilde{\chi}_{k}^{\prime}(t)\rho_{(k,n)}

(143)

where
\rho
is a trace-free double anti-divergence of
w_{(k,n)}
. From
\mu=N^{1/2}\Xi^{3/2}D_{R}^{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|R_{(n+1)}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\tau}\max_{k}\|\rho_{(k,n)}\|_{0}\leq\frac{C}{\tau}S_{\rho}=\frac{C}{\tau}(\log\widehat{\Xi})\mu^{-1}D_{R,n}

\displaystyle\|R_{(n+1)}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C(\log\widehat{\Xi})^{2}(D_{u}/D_{R})^{1/2}N^{-1/2}D_{R,n}

\displaystyle\|R_{(n+1)}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq(\log\widehat{\Xi})^{-1}D_{R,n+1}

where in the last line we use the lower bound on
N
given by (
7
),
(\log\widehat{\Xi})^{3}\lesssim_{\eta}N^{\eta}\Xi^{\eta}
and we take
\widehat{C}
sufficiently large. This proves (
75
) with a log of room to spare.

The proof of (
76
) is a simpler version of the proof of (
77
), so we prove only the latter. Notice that
(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}\leq(|\vec{a}|+1-L)_{+}+3
since
{\underline{L}}=L-3
. We then obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}R_{(n+1)}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{k}\|\tilde{\chi}_{k}^{\prime\prime}(t)\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\rho_{(k,n)}\|_{C^{0}}+\sup_{k}\|\tilde{\chi}_{k}^{\prime}(t)\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\rho_{(k,n)}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau^{-2}[\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\log\widehat{\Xi})\mu^{-1}D_{R,n}]+\tau^{-1}[\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\tau^{-1}(\log\widehat{\Xi})\mu^{-1}D_{R,n}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}[\widehat{N}^{3}\tau^{-2}(\log\widehat{\Xi})\mu^{-1}]D_{R,n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})\frac{D_{R,n}}{N^{\eta}\Xi^{\eta}}=\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-L)_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})D_{R,n+1}

where in the last line we recall
\mu^{-1}=N^{-1/2}\Xi^{-3/2}D_{R}^{-1/2}
,
\tau^{-1}=b^{-1}(\log\widehat{\Xi})\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}=b^{-1}(\log\widehat{\Xi})\Xi^{3/2}D_{u}^{1/2}
,
\widehat{N}^{3}=N^{3/L}
and we use the lower bound (
7
) on
N
and the estimate
(\log\widehat{\Xi})^{3}\lesssim_{\eta}N^{\eta}\Xi^{\eta}
.
∎

3.8
Errors after the Newton step

Upon completing the Newton step we have new errors described by (
38
)-(
41
). The error term in (
38
) has already been estimated. Let us now estimate the terms in (
40
)-(
41
).

We introduce the notation
R_{M,n}
and
R_{Q,n}
to denote special solutions to

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\nabla_{\ell}R_{M,n}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle=T^{\ell}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})\nabla_{\ell}w_{n}+T^{\ell}w_{n}\nabla_{\ell}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})

(144)

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\nabla_{\ell}R_{Q,n}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle=T^{\ell}w_{n}\nabla_{\ell}w_{n}+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(T^{\ell}w_{n}\nabla_{\ell}w_{j}+T^{\ell}w_{j}\nabla_{\ell}w_{n})

(145)

Lemma 3.7
.

For
0\leq|\vec{a}|+r\leq L
and
0\leq r\leq 1

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})\|_{C^{0}}+\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}(u-u_{\epsilon})\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}(\accentset{\ast}{\Xi}\accentset{\ast}{e}_{u}^{1/2})^{r}N^{-1}e_{u}^{1/2},

(146)

Furthermore, for all
0\leq r\leq 1
and all
\vec{a}
one has

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}w_{n}\|_{C^{0}}+\Xi\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}z_{n}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\mu^{r}S_{w}.

(147)

Proof.

Recall that
\theta_{\epsilon}=P_{\leq q_{\epsilon}}\theta
and
u_{\epsilon}^{\ell}=T^{\ell}\theta_{\epsilon}
, where
q_{\epsilon}
is chosen such that
2^{q_{\epsilon}}\sim\epsilon^{-1}=N^{1/L}\Xi
. We begin by estimating the difference
\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}
:

\displaystyle\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\theta-P_{\leq q_{\epsilon}}\theta=P_{>q_{\epsilon}}\theta.

When we bound
\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}
in
C^{0}
, we need to be very precise and use the fact that the moments

\int h^{\vec{a}}\eta_{\epsilon}(h)\,dh=0

all vanish for
0<|a|\leq L
. This implies

\|\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{0}\lesssim\epsilon^{L}\|\nabla^{L}\theta\|_{0}\lesssim\frac{e_{u}^{1/2}}{N}.

Now we move on to the
|a|\geq 1
case.

Now we consider
1\leq|\vec{a}|+r\leq L
. We use a trivial bound of

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}\theta\|_{C^{0}}+\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}\theta_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

(148)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})^{r}e_{u}^{1/2}=\Xi^{|\vec{a}|+3r/2}D_{u}^{(r+1)/2}

(149)

Our goal is to bound this expression by

\displaystyle(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}(\accentset{\ast}{\Xi}\accentset{\ast}{e}_{u}^{1/2})^{r}D_{u}^{1/2}/N

\displaystyle=(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}(N\Xi)^{3r/2}D_{R}^{r/2}D_{u}^{1/2}/N

(150)

Thus we must check that for
1\leq|\vec{a}|+r\leq L
we have

\displaystyle(D_{u}/D_{R})^{r/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{3r/2+|\vec{a}|-1}=N^{r/2+(r+|\vec{a}|-1)}

(151)

This lower bound follows from (
7
), which implies
N\geq D_{u}/D_{R}
. The same proof applies to
u
since we have assumed the same bounds on
u
as for
\theta
.

To prove (
147
), recall that
w_{n}=\sum_{k}\tilde{\chi}_{k}w_{k,n}
, with
w_{k,n}=\tilde{w}_{k,n}+\bar{w}_{k,n}
, each of size bounded by
S_{w}
. In order to estimate
\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}w_{k,n}
, it suffices to observe that:

•

Taking a spatial derivative never costs more than
\widehat{N}\Xi\leq N\Xi
.

•

Taking an advective derivative
\overline{D}_{t}
of
\bar{w}_{k,n}
costs at most
\tau^{-1}\ll\mu
.

•

Taking a pure time derivative
\partial_{t}=\overline{D}_{t}-u_{\epsilon}\cdot\nabla
of either
\tilde{w}_{k,n}
or
\bar{w}_{k,n}
costs at most
\mu
.

Similar considerations hold for
z_{k,n}
, which has size
S_{z}=\Xi^{-1}S_{w}
.
∎

Proposition 3.7
.

For appropriately chosen
R_{M,n}
and
R_{Q,n}
, we have the estimates

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}R_{M,n}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}N^{-1}D_{R}

(152)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}R_{Q,n}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}S_{w}^{2}\Xi^{-1}\lesssim(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}N^{-1}D_{R}

(153)

for
0\leq|\vec{a}|\leq L
and

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}R_{M,n}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-1}N^{-1}D_{R}

(154)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}R_{Q,n}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-1}S_{w}^{2}\Xi^{-1}\lesssim(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-1}N^{-1}D_{R}

(155)

for
0\leq|\vec{a}|\leq L-1
. Here
\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-1}=(N\Xi)^{3/2}D_{R}^{1/2}
, and
S_{w}
is as documented in the table (
3.7
), namely
S_{w}=\Xi^{2}\mu^{-1}D_{R,n}
,
\mu=N^{1/2}\Xi^{3/2}D_{R}^{1/2}
.

The quadratic terms
R_{Q,n}
.

We begin by estimating an inverse double divergence of
T^{\ell}w_{n}\nabla_{\ell}w_{n}=\nabla_{\ell}(w_{n}T^{\ell}w_{n})
. It suffices to only estimate this term since the other terms in the equation for
R_{Q,n}
are similar.

We must estimate a solution to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla_{j}R^{j\ell}_{Q,n}&=T^{\ell}w_{n}w_{n}\\
&=\sum_{q}P_{\leq q-1}w_{J}T^{\ell}P_{q+1}w_{J}\\
&\quad+P_{q+1}w_{J}P_{\leq q-1}T^{\ell}w_{J}\\
&\quad+P_{q+1}w_{J}T^{\ell}P_{q}w_{J}+P_{q}w_{J}T^{\ell}P_{q+1}w_{J}+P_{q+1}w_{J}T^{\ell}P_{q+1}w_{J}.\end{split}

(156)

Specifically, we achieve bounds for

\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}R_{Q,n}^{\ell}=\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}\operatorname{div}^{-1}(w_{n}T^{\ell}w_{n}).

We decompose this as
LH+HL+HH
in the manner of (
156
).

For brevity, we omit
n
in the subscript.

Terms
R_{QHL}
and
R_{QLH}
.

The low-high terms are analogous to the high-low terms; thus, we concentrate our analysis on the latter. Its
q
’th frequency component is

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}R_{QHLq}^{j\ell}=\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}[P_{q+1}w_{J}T^{a}P_{\leq q-1}w_{J}].

(157)

We select
\bar{q}
such that
2^{\bar{q}}\sim\Xi
.

Consider the case
q\leq\bar{q}
. In this case we express
w_{J}=\nabla_{i}\nabla_{b}r_{J}^{ib}
in the rightmost copy of
w_{J}
and bound the operator norm of
TP_{\leq q-1}\nabla\nabla
. By doing so, we obtain:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}R_{QHLq}^{j\ell}

\displaystyle=\overset{\sim}{\sum}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}[\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\partial_{t}^{r_{1}}P_{q+1}w_{J}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\partial_{t}^{r_{2}}T^{a}P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{b}r_{J}^{ib}]

(158)

\displaystyle\|\eqref{eq:QuadHLlow}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}\|\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\partial_{t}^{r_{1}}P_{q+1}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|T^{a}P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{b}\|\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\partial_{t}^{r_{2}}r_{J}^{ib}\|_{C^{0}}

(159)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-q}2^{2q}\overset{\sim}{\sum}(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}\mu^{r_{1}}S_{w}(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}\mu^{r_{2}}S_{r}

(160)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{q}\Xi^{-2}(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\mu^{r}S_{w}^{2}

(161)

For high frequencies
q\geq\bar{q}
, we first prove the preliminary
bound

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}P_{\leq q}T^{a}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}P_{\leq\bar{q}}T^{a}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}+\sum_{q=\bar{q}}^{\infty}\|T^{a}P_{q}\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|P_{\leq\bar{q}}T^{a}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{b}\|\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}r_{J}^{ib}\|_{C^{0}}+\sum_{q=\bar{q}}^{\infty}2^{-q}\|\nabla\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{2\bar{q}}\Xi^{-2}(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\mu^{r}S_{w}+2^{-\bar{q}}\Xi(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\mu^{r}S_{w}

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}P_{\leq q}T^{a}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\mu^{r}S_{w}

(162)

We now apply this estimate to

\displaystyle\|\eqref{eq:qQuadHLterm}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}\|\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\partial_{t}^{r_{1}}P_{q+1}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\partial_{t}^{r_{2}}P_{\leq q-1}T^{a}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}2^{-q}[(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}\mu^{r_{1}}S_{w}][(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}\mu^{r_{2}}S_{w}]

(163)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-q}(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\mu^{r}S_{w}^{2}.

(164)

Summing (
161
) over
q<\bar{q}
and (
164
) over
q\geq\bar{q}
yields (
155
) for
R_{QHL}
.

Term
R_{QHH}.

We decompose the high-high frequency interactions into three parts: those with the operators applied in the order
P_{q+1},P_{q}
; those with the order reversed; and those involving both
P_{q+1}
.

We begin with the third group of terms. We can consider the other two terms similarly, as a single group. Note that we don’t consider them separately because we need to consider those two together in order to get an anti-divergence. For brevity, we only demonstrate the part with both operators being
P_{q+1}.

We need to bound

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}K_{q1}^{j\ell}\ast[w_{J},w_{J}]=\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}\int P_{q+1}w_{J}(x-h_{1})P_{q+1}w_{J}(x-h_{2})K_{q1}^{j\ell}(h_{1},h_{2})dh_{1}dh_{2}.

(165)

Note that we can distribute the derivatives inside the integral using the product rule. We first consider the case where
q>\bar{q}
.

\displaystyle\|\eqref{eq:bilinConvFormq}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|K_{q}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\partial_{t}^{r_{1}}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\partial_{t}^{r_{2}}w_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

(166)

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}2^{-q}(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\mu^{r}S_{w}^{2}

(167)

For
q\leq\bar{q}
, we write
w_{J}=\nabla_{i}z_{J}^{i}
and integrate by parts to find

\displaystyle K_{q1}^{j\ell}\ast[w_{J},w_{J}]

\displaystyle=\int z_{J}^{a}(x-h_{1})z_{J}^{b}(x-h_{2})\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}K_{q}^{j\ell}(h_{1},h_{2})dh_{1}dh_{2}

(168)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}^{r}\eqref{eq:integratedByParts}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\overset{\sim}{\sum}\|\nabla^{2}K_{q}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\partial_{t}^{r_{1}}z_{J}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\partial_{t}^{r_{2}}z_{J}\|_{C^{0}}

(169)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{q}(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\mu^{r}S_{z}^{2}\sim 2^{q}\Xi^{-2}(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\mu^{r}S_{w}^{2}

(170)

Now we sum (
167
) over
q>\bar{q}
and (
170
) over
q\leq\bar{q}
to obtain (
155
) for
R_{QHH}
.

The mollification terms
R_{M,n}
.

Recall that
R_{M,n}
solves

\nabla_{j}\nabla_{\ell}R_{M,n}^{j\ell}=T^{\ell}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})\nabla_{\ell}w_{n}+T^{\ell}w_{n}\nabla_{\ell}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}).

Here, by definition,
\theta_{\epsilon}:=P_{\leq\hat{q}}\theta.
Thus
\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}
only has frequencies above
2^{\hat{q}}
. The idea is to expand these terms and observe that every single one of the
\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}
terms is of high frequency
>2^{\hat{q}}
. Thus

\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}=P_{>\hat{q}}\theta.

We have

\nabla_{j}R_{M,n}^{j\ell}=\sum_{J(n)}\left((\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})T^{\ell}w_{J}+w_{J}T^{\ell}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})\right)\tilde{\chi}_{k}(t).

For simplicity we write

\nabla_{j}R_{M,n}^{j\ell}\simeq(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})T^{\ell}w_{J}+w_{J}T^{\ell}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}).

From now on, we will suppress the
\tilde{\chi}_{k}(t)
and summation notation.

We have

R^{j\ell}_{M,n}=R_{MLH,n}^{j\ell}+R_{MHL,n}^{j\ell}+R_{MHH,n}^{j\ell}.

Taking spatial and time derivatives of the LH term, we have:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}}R^{j\ell}_{MLHq,n}

\displaystyle=\overset{\sim}{\sum}\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}[\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}P_{\leq q-1}T^{a}P_{>\hat{q}}\theta\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}P_{q+1}w_{J}]

\displaystyle=\overset{\sim}{\sum}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}[\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}P_{\leq q-1}T^{a}P_{>\hat{q}}\theta\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}P_{q+1}w_{J}]

\displaystyle\quad+\overset{\sim}{\sum}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}[\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}P_{\leq q-1}T^{a}P_{>\hat{q}}\theta\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}P_{q+1}\partial_{t}w_{J}]

Taking spatial and time derivatives of the HL term, we have:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}}R^{j\ell}_{MHLq,n}

\displaystyle=\overset{\sim}{\sum}\partial_{t}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}[\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}P_{q+1}P_{>\hat{q}}\theta T^{a}P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}w_{J}]

\displaystyle=\overset{\sim}{\sum}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}[\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}P_{q+1}P_{>\hat{q}}\theta T^{a}P_{\leq q-1}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}(w_{J})]

\displaystyle\quad+\overset{\sim}{\sum}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}[\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}P_{q+1}P_{>\hat{q}}\theta T^{a}P_{\leq q-1}\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}w_{J}].

We can obtain a similar expression for the derivatives of
R_{MHHq,n}
, which for conciseness we omit.

The term
R_{MHH}.

We have

\nabla_{j}R^{j\ell}_{MHHq,n}=P_{q+1}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})T^{\ell}P_{q}w_{J}+P_{q}w_{J}T^{\ell}P_{q+1}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}).

We must treat both terms together (rather than only one of the two terms at a time), since there is no anti-divergence if these two terms are separated from each other.

We have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{r}\nabla_{\vec{a}}R^{j\ell}_{MHHq,n}

\displaystyle=\overset{\sim}{\sum}\int\partial_{t}^{r_{1}}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}(\theta(x-h_{1})-\theta_{\epsilon}(x-h_{1}))\partial_{t}^{r_{2}}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}w_{J}(x-h_{2})K_{q}^{j\ell}(h_{1},h_{2})\,dh_{1}dh_{2}

\displaystyle=\overset{\sim}{\sum}\int\partial_{t}^{r_{1}}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}(P_{\approx q}[\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}](x-h_{1}))\partial_{t}^{r_{2}}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}w_{J}(x-h_{2})K_{q}^{j\ell}(h_{1},h_{2})\,dh_{1}dh_{2}

We can bound each term as follows:

1. For the first term, we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\int\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}(P_{\approx q}[\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}])\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}w_{J}K_{q}^{j\ell}\,dh_{1}dh_{2}\right\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|K_{q}^{j\ell}\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}(P_{\approx q}[\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}])\|_{0}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}w_{J}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-q}[(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}(\accentset{\ast}{\Xi}\accentset{\ast}{e}_{u}^{1/2})N^{-1}e_{u}^{1/2}][(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}S_{w}]

2. For the second term, we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\int\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}(P_{\approx q}[\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}])\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}w_{J}K_{q}^{j\ell}\,dh_{1}dh_{2}\right\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|K_{q}^{j\ell}\|_{1}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}(P_{\approx q}[\theta-\theta_{\epsilon}])\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}w_{J}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim[2^{-q}][(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}N^{-1}e_{u}^{1/2}][(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}\mu S_{w}]

The sum of these terms is bounded by

[2^{-q}]\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-1}[(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}N^{-1}e_{u}^{1/2}][(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}S_{w}]

where
\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-1}
is the inverse timescale

\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-1}:=(N\Xi)^{3/2}D_{R}^{1/2}.

Now, summing over
q>\hat{q}-1
, we get
\sum 2^{-q}\sim\widehat{\Xi}^{-1}
and:

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}}R^{j\ell}_{MHH,n}\|\lesssim\sum_{q}\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{\vec{a}}R^{j\ell}_{MHHq,n}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{\Xi}^{-1}\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-1}N^{|\vec{a}|-1}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{w}

More generally,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{r}\nabla_{\vec{a}}R^{j\ell}_{MHH,n}\|\lesssim\widehat{\Xi}^{-1}\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-r}N^{|\vec{a}|-1}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{w}

The terms
R_{MHL}
and
R_{MLH}
.

Our first group of terms is

R_{MHL1,n}^{j\ell}=\sum_{q\geq\hat{q}-1}\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}\left[P_{\leq q-1}T^{a}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})P_{q+1}w_{J}\right].

As usual, we add a subscript
q
to label each term in the sum. So, for
R_{MHL1,n}
, we’ll call the individual pieces
R_{MHL1q,n}
.

R_{MHL1q,n}:=\mathcal{R}^{j\ell}_{a}P_{\approx q}\left[P_{\leq q-1}T^{a}(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})P_{q+1}w_{J}\right]

For
0\leq r+|\vec{a}|\leq L
, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{r}\nabla_{\vec{a}}R_{MHL1q,n}^{j\ell}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{R}P_{\approx q}\|_{op}\|\partial_{t}^{r_{1}}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}P_{\leq q-1}T(\theta-\theta_{\epsilon})\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}^{r_{2}}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}P_{q+1}w_{J}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim[2^{-q}][\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-r_{1}}(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}N^{-1}e_{u}^{1/2}][\mu^{r_{2}}(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}_{2}|}S_{w}]

Thus, since
\mu\leq\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-1}
,

\|\partial_{t}^{r}\nabla_{\vec{a}}R_{MHL1,n}^{j\ell}\|_{0}\lesssim\widehat{\Xi}^{-1}\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-r}N^{|\vec{a}|-1}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{w}\leq(N\Xi)^{|\vec{a}|}\accentset{\ast}{\tau}^{-r}[(N\Xi)^{-1}e_{u}^{1/2}S_{w}]

We would like the expression in brackets to be bounded by
CD_{R}/N
, which is indeed the case. One can check this by recalling that
e_{u}^{1/2}=\Xi^{1/2}D_{u}^{1/2}
, while

S_{w}\leq\mu^{-1}\Xi^{2}D_{R}=N^{-1/2}\Xi^{-3/2+2}D_{R}^{1/2}\leq D_{u}^{-1/2}\Xi^{1/2}D_{R}

thanks to (
7
), which implies
N\geq D_{u}/D_{R}
.

The bounds for
\partial_{t}^{r}\nabla_{\vec{a}}R^{j\ell}_{MLHq,n}
are similar to the bounds for
\partial_{t}^{r}\nabla_{\vec{a}}R^{j\ell}_{MHLq,n}
.

4
Convex integration

Define the representative index set
{\mathcal{I}}_{+}:=\mathbb{F}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\{0,\ldots,\Gamma-1\}
and the full index set
{\mathcal{I}}:={\mathcal{I}}_{+}\cup\{\bar{I}:I\in{\mathcal{I}}_{+}\}
, where the conjugate index is defined by
\overline{(f,k,n)}=(-f,k,n)
. Thus every conjugate pair is represented exactly once by an index
I=(f,k,n)\in{\mathcal{I}}_{+}
. The last index corresponds to precisely the
\Gamma
stages
n=0,\ldots,\Gamma-1
appearing in the recurrence for
S_{(\Gamma)}
. Choose
\lambda\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}
such that
\lambda\sim N\Xi
. The oscillatory wave has the form

\displaystyle\Theta

\displaystyle=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}}\Theta_{I},\qquad\Theta_{I}=g_{[f,k,n]}(\mu t)P_{I}[e^{i\lambda\xi_{I}}\theta_{I}]

(171)

\displaystyle\theta_{I}

\displaystyle=(2\lambda)^{1/2}\gamma_{I},\qquad\gamma_{(f,k,n)}=\chi_{k}e_{n}^{1/2}(t)\gamma_{f}\left(p_{I}\right)

(172)

\displaystyle\check{p}_{I}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{R_{(n)}^{j\ell}}{M_{e}D_{R,n}},\nabla\check{\xi}_{k}\right)

(173)

\displaystyle p_{I}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\tilde{R}_{(n)}^{j\ell}}{M_{e}D_{R,n}},\nabla\xi_{k}\right)

(174)

where
P_{I}
is a frequency localization operator whose symbol, which is defined via scaling, is equal to
1
on a
\lambda|f|/5
-neighborhood of
\lambda f
and is supported in a
\lambda|f|/3
-neighborhood of
\lambda f
. With these definitions, the conjugate wave indexed by
\bar{I}=(-f,k,n)
satisfies

\Theta_{\bar{I}}=\overline{\Theta_{I}}
,
\xi_{\bar{I}}=-\xi_{I}
,
g_{[\bar{I}]}=g_{[I]}
, and the symbol of
P_{\bar{I}}
is a reflection of that of
P_{I}
.

We will use mollification to define
\widetilde{R}_{(n)}
. We postpone for now the necessary estimates on
\widetilde{R}_{(n)}
and
\nabla\xi_{I}
that ensure the construction is well-defined. In particular, we will have to show that
\widetilde{R}_{(n)}
and
\nabla\xi_{I}
do not escape the domains of
\gamma_{f}
and
B^{j\ell}
. We will also require that
e_{n}(t)=M_{e}D_{R,n}
on the support of
\tilde{R}_{(n)}
, and that
\lambda\nabla\xi_{I}
takes values in the set where the symbol of
P_{I}
equals
1
.

Notice that, by construction and the disjointness of supports of the functions
g_{[f,k,n]}
, we have the crucial disjointness property

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp}_{t}\,\Theta_{I}\cap\mathrm{supp}_{t}\,\Theta_{J}

\displaystyle=\emptyset\qquad\mbox{ if }I\notin\{J,\bar{J}\}

(175)

Now let
\tilde{\theta}_{\epsilon}=\theta_{\epsilon}+w=\theta_{\epsilon}+\sum_{n=0}^{\Gamma-1}w_{n}
and
\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}^{\ell}=T^{\ell}\tilde{\theta}_{\epsilon}=u_{\epsilon}^{\ell}+T^{\ell}w
.

We obtain the following estimates for
\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}
.

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{a}}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}e_{u}^{1/2},\qquad\qquad\qquad

(176)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\overline{D}_{t}\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{a}}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})e_{u}^{1/2},

(177)

Notice that these are the same estimates that hold for
u_{\epsilon}
except that the losses of powers of
\widehat{N}
occur earlier (after
{\underline{L}}
derivatives rather than
L
). These bounds follow from (
73
).

We will also need a bound on the advective derivative of
\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}
along its own flow. Setting
\tilde{D}_{t}=\partial_{t}+\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}\cdot\nabla
, the following bound suffices:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{a}}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})N^{(|\vec{a}|+1-L)_{+}/L}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}e_{u}^{1/2}.

(178)

This bound is a corollary of (
176
)-(
177
) and the following Lemma, which is generally useful when converting bounds between different time and advective derivatives.

Lemma 4.1

(Interchange Lemma)
.

Let
\mathring{D}_{t}
be one of the operators
\mathring{D}_{t}\in\{\partial_{t},\overline{D}_{t},\tilde{D}_{t}\}
. Consider any inverse timescale
\zeta\geq\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}
. Define the weighted norm of a smooth tensor field
F
by

\displaystyle\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F]

\displaystyle=\max_{0\leq r\leq 1}\max_{0\leq|\vec{a}|+r\leq L^{\prime}}\frac{\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\mathring{D}_{t}^{r}F\|_{C^{0}}}{\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+r-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\zeta^{r}}

(179)

If
\zeta
is omitted in the notation, set
\mathring{H}[F]=\mathring{H}_{\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}}
.

Then there exist constants depending only on
L^{\prime}
such that

\tilde{H}_{\zeta}[F]\lesssim\bar{H}_{\zeta}[F]\lesssim H^{\partial_{t}}_{\zeta}[F]\lesssim\tilde{H}_{\zeta}[F].

Proof.

We show only that
H^{\partial_{t}}_{\zeta}[F]\lesssim\tilde{H}_{\zeta}[F]
as the other directions are similar

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}F

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{D}_{t}F-\nabla_{\vec{a}}[\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}^{i}\nabla_{i}F]

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}F\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\zeta\tilde{H}[F]+\sum_{|\vec{a}_{1}|+|\vec{a}_{2}|=|\vec{a}|}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{1}}\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}\|_{C^{0}}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{2}}\nabla_{i}F\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\zeta\tilde{H}[F]

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\vec{a}_{1}|+|\vec{a}_{2}|=|\vec{a}|}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{1}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{1}|}e_{u}^{1/2}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{2}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{2}|+1}\tilde{H}[F]

We now apply the counting inequality
(x-z)_{+}+(y-z)_{+}\leq(x+y-z)_{+}
,
x,y,z\geq 0
with
x=|\vec{a}_{1}|
,
y=|\vec{a}_{2}|+1
,
z={\underline{L}}\geq 0
, and recall
\zeta\geq\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}
, to obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\partial_{t}F\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\zeta\tilde{H}[F],

which is the desired estimate after dividing through by the prefactor of
\tilde{H}[F]
.
∎

We will also use the following chain rule and product rule

Proposition 4.1

(Chain rule and product rule for weighted norms)
.

Consider the operators
\mathring{D}_{t}\in\{\partial_{t},\overline{D}_{t},\tilde{D}_{t}\}
and let
F
be
C^{\infty}
. Let
G
be a
C^{\infty}
function defined on a compact neighborhood of the image of
F
. Then

\displaystyle\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[G(F)]

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F])^{L^{\prime}}

(180)

\displaystyle\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{1}F_{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{1}]\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{2}]

(181)

with implicit constants depending on
L^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We compute for
0\leq r\leq 1
,
0\leq r+|\vec{a}|\leq L^{\prime}

\displaystyle\nabla_{\vec{a}}\mathring{D}_{t}^{r}G(F)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{|\vec{a}|+r}\overset{\sim}{\sum}\partial^{k}G(F)\prod_{i=0}^{k}\nabla_{\vec{a}_{i}}\mathring{D}_{t}^{r_{i}}F

where the sum is over appropriate indices such that
\sum_{i}|\vec{a}_{i}|=|\vec{a}|
and
\sum_{i}r_{i}=r
. Then

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\mathring{D}_{t}^{r}G(F)\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{|\vec{a}|+r}\|\partial^{k}G\|_{C^{0}}\prod_{i=0}^{k}\|\nabla_{\vec{a}_{i}}\mathring{D}_{t}^{r_{i}}F\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{|\vec{a}|+r}\prod_{i=0}^{k}\left[\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}_{i}|+r_{i}-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}_{i}|}\zeta^{r_{i}}\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F]\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+r-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\zeta^{r}(1+\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F])^{L^{\prime}},

which is the desired bound.

The product rule can be proven by direct computation, but it can also be deduced from the Chain Rule as follows. The vector-valued function
(\frac{F_{1}}{\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{1}]},\frac{F_{2}}{\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{2}]})
takes values in
\{(u,v):\max\{\|u\|,\|v\|\}\leq 1\}
and
G(u,v)=uv
is smooth in a compact neighborhood of this set. We then have by the chain rule

\displaystyle\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{1}F_{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{1}]\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{2}]\mathring{H}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{1}}{\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{1}]}\frac{F_{2}}{\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{2}]}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{1}]\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{2}]\left(1+\mathring{H}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{1}}{\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{1}]}\right]+\mathring{H}_{\zeta}\left[\frac{F_{2}}{\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{2}]}\right]\right)^{L^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{1}]\mathring{H}_{\zeta}[F_{2}].

∎

Proposition 4.2
.

For
\mathring{D}_{t}\in\{\partial_{t},\overline{D}_{t},\tilde{D}_{t}\}
define the prime weighted norm

\displaystyle\mathring{H}^{\prime}_{\zeta}[F]=\max_{0\leq r\leq 1}\max_{0\leq|\vec{a}|+r\leq L^{\prime}}\frac{\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\mathring{D}_{t}F\|_{C^{0}}}{\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+r-({\underline{L}}-1))_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}\zeta^{r}}

(182)

Then the natural analogues of Lemma
4.1
and Proposition
4.1
hold for the prime weighted norms as well.

We omit the proof, which is essentially the same as that of Lemma
4.1
and Proposition
4.1
.

We are now ready to define
\tilde{R}_{(n)}
. We mollify along the flow at the scale
\epsilon_{t}
from (
17
), which is defined using the natural stress timescale, rather than mollifying in time at fixed spatial points. This allows the estimates from earlier work to apply directly. Set

\displaystyle\tilde{R}_{(n)}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}\ast_{\Phi}R_{(n)}=\int R_{(n)}(\Phi_{s}(t,x))\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}(s)ds

(183)

where
\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}(s)=\epsilon_{t}^{-1}\eta(s/\epsilon_{t})\geq 0
is a standard mollifying kernel supported in
|s|<\epsilon_{t}
and where
\Phi_{s}(t)
is the flow map of
\partial_{t}+\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}\cdot\nabla
, which is the unique solution to

\displaystyle\Phi_{s}(t,x)

\displaystyle=(t+s,\Phi_{s}^{1}(t,x),\Phi_{s}^{2}(t,x)),

(184)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{ds}\Phi_{s}^{i}

\displaystyle=\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}^{i}(\Phi_{s}(t,x))\qquad i=1,2

(185)

\displaystyle\Phi_{0}(t,x)

\displaystyle=(t,x).

(186)

This is the flow convolution defined in
[
35
, Section 18.2, equations (617)–(618)]
. We adapt the estimates of
[
35
, Sections 18.4–18.6]
to our parameters. In this comparison, the source quantities
R_{\epsilon_{x}}
,
R_{\epsilon}
,
e_{R}
,
v_{\epsilon}
, and
\overline{D}/\partial t
correspond to our
R_{(n)}
,
\tilde{R}_{(n)}
,
D_{R,n}
,
\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}
, and
\tilde{D}_{t}
, respectively.

With this definition we can check that the coefficients
\gamma_{(f,k,n)}
are well-defined. To see this claim, first observe that, since
\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}\geq 0
, we have
\|\tilde{R}_{(n)}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\|R_{(n)}\|_{C^{0}}\leq A_{L}D_{R,(n)}
for all
n
. Furthermore, since mollification along the flow grows the time support by at most
\epsilon_{t}\leq\tau
, we also have by construction that
e_{n}(t)=M_{e}D_{R,(n)}
on the support of
\tilde{R}_{(n)}
. Hence the construction of the coefficient
\gamma_{(f,k,n)}
is well-defined. We now need estimates for
\tilde{R}_{(n)}
and its spatial and advective derivatives.

The Newton estimates (
76
)–(
77
) are stated with
\overline{D}_{t}
. Since
\tilde{D}_{t}=\overline{D}_{t}+(Tw)\cdot\nabla
, Lemma
4.1
and the Newton-velocity bounds give, for
n\geq 1
,

\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{D}_{t}R_{(n)}\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-\underline{L})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})D_{R,n}.

The same interchange lemma using the bounds (
66
)-(
67
) yields the above estimate for
R_{(0)}=R_{\epsilon}
. Thus the flow-mollification estimates of
[
35
, Sections 18.4–18.6]
apply uniformly with stress level
D_{R,n}
and loss-free order
\underline{L}=L-3
.

Proposition 4.3

(Stress estimates)
.

\displaystyle\|R_{(n)}-\tilde{R}_{(n)}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{t}\|\tilde{D}_{t}R_{(n)}\|_{C^{0}}\lesssim(D_{u}/D_{R})^{-1/2}N^{-1/2}D_{R,n}

(187)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{R}_{(n)}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{a}}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}D_{R,n}

(188)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{D}_{t}\tilde{R}_{(n)}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{a}}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}D_{R,n}

(189)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}\tilde{R}_{(n)}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{a}}\epsilon_{t}^{-1}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}D_{R,n}.

(190)

The first line is also immediate from

\displaystyle\tilde{R}_{(n)}-R_{(n)}

\displaystyle=\int\left[R_{(n)}(\Phi_{s}(t,x))-R_{(n)}(t,x)\right]\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}(s)ds,

the fundamental theorem of calculus along
\Phi_{s}
and

\int|s|\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}(s)\,ds\lesssim\epsilon_{t}.

The reason for the appearance of
\epsilon_{t}^{-1}
in the last estimate is the appearance of
\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}^{\prime}(s)
in following calculation

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{t}\int R_{(n)}(\Phi_{s}(t,x))\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}(s)ds

\displaystyle=\int\frac{d}{ds}R_{(n)}(\Phi_{s}(t,x))\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}(s)ds=-\int R_{(n)}(\Phi_{s}(t,x))\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}^{\prime}(s)ds

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}\int R_{(n)}(\Phi_{s}(t,x))\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}(s)ds

\displaystyle=-\int\tilde{D}_{t}R_{(n)}(\Phi_{s}(t,x))\eta_{\epsilon_{t}}^{\prime}(s)ds

Thus, the second advective derivative costs
\|\eta^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{t}}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\epsilon_{t}^{-1}
. We retain the stage-relative factor
D_{R,n}
for the
\Gamma
active stages
n=0,\ldots,\Gamma-1
.

We define the phase functions
\xi_{I}
to solve

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}^{j}\nabla_{j})\xi_{I}

\displaystyle=0

(191)

\displaystyle\xi_{(f,k,n)}(k\tau,x)

\displaystyle=\check{\xi}_{(f,k)}(k\tau,x)=f\cdot x

(192)

Notice that
\xi_{I}
and
\check{\xi}_{I}
have the same initial data but differ in terms of which vector field transports them.

We obtain the following estimates for
\xi_{I}
:

Proposition 4.4

(Phase function estimates)
.

The phase functions satisfy the following bounds on the interval
[t(I)-\tau,t(I)+\tau]

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\nabla\xi_{I}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{a}}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|},

(193)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{D}_{t}\nabla\xi_{I}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{a}}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+1-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2}),

(194)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\vec{a}}\tilde{D}_{t}^{2}\nabla\xi_{I}\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{a}}\widehat{N}^{(|\vec{a}|+2-{\underline{L}})_{+}}\Xi^{|\vec{a}|}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})^{2}

(195)

Proof.

These estimates are derived by differentiating the transport equation (
191
) and applying Gronwall to the weighted jet of
\xi_{I}
. Propositions 17.3 and 17.5 and Propositions 17.6–17.7 of
[
35
]
give, respectively, the spatial, first material derivative, and second material derivative estimates. The inputs are exactly (
176
)–(
178
) and the restriction
|t-t(I)|\leq\tau\lesssim(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})^{-1}
. To match notation, take the source loss-free order
L
to be our
\underline{L}=L-3
, denote the source factor
N^{1/L}
by
\widehat{N}
, and replace the source
v_{\epsilon}
,
e_{v}^{1/2}
, and
\overline{D}/\partial t
by
\tilde{u}_{\epsilon}
,
e_{u}^{1/2}
, and
\tilde{D}_{t}
. For example, the source factor
N^{(m-L)_{+}/L}
becomes
\widehat{N}^{(m-\underline{L})_{+}}
, which is the loss appearing in the displayed bounds.
∎

We will need a good estimate on how close the phase gradients are to those that were used in the Newton step. The equation we need to analyze is

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u_{\epsilon}^{j}\nabla_{j})(\check{\xi}_{J}-\xi_{J})

\displaystyle=T^{j}w\nabla_{j}\xi_{J}

(196)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u_{\epsilon}^{j}\nabla_{j})(\nabla_{a}\check{\xi}_{J}-\nabla_{a}\xi_{J})

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{a}(T^{j}w\nabla_{j}\xi_{J})-\nabla_{a}u_{\epsilon}^{j}\nabla_{j}(\check{\xi}_{J}-\xi_{J})

(197)

Again, the initial data for
\nabla\check{\xi}_{J}
and
\nabla\xi_{J}
are equal at time
t(I)
. From this equation, we use the fact that the time scale
\tau\leq(\log\widehat{\Xi})^{-1}(\Xi e_{u}^{1/2})^{-1}
, and apply the method of characteristics and Gronwall to obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla\check{\xi}(t)-\nabla\xi_{I}(t)\|_{0}
